Detail below, newest first.

### 🐞 2026-09-25 — the C1 instrument was crashing a process, and the fault counter was double-counting

Two defects in my own measuring apparatus, found by addr2line'ing a fault I had already
(wrongly) written off as "not a regression, ntpclient has crashed before".

**1. The guard was the faulting instruction** (libphoenix `819e461`). `c1hpa01` showed an EL0
Data Abort at `pc=0x41ed00`, `far=0x5000`. Symbolised against the unstripped `prog/psh` —
identity-checked, its stripped twin is byte-identical to the binary that ran — that is
`malloc_heapSizeValid` → `malloc_liveOverlap` → `_malloc_heapAlloc`, i.e. **my C1-hunt code**.
The function's comment claimed it "vets the pointer before dereferencing it, which is the whole
reason it exists"; the comment on the `heapLo/heapHi` fields thirty lines up says the window is
"only ever widened" and is "a plausibility filter, **not exact membership**". Both were in the
file; the false one was believed. A released heap leaves a **hole** inside the window, so
`0x5000` (register `x19` held it, matching `far` exactly) passed every test and faulted.
Fixed with a real mapping test — `va2pa()` is `pmap_resolve()`, which returns 0 for an invalid
descriptor — kept **out** of the hot coalesce path, and a violation now **prints** a tagged line
instead of skipping silently. `malloc_c1Scan` had the same deref with *no* guard at all, on a
timer over 256 slots.

**2. Every EL0 fault count was 2×** (`ef51c9e4`). `process_dumpException` (kernel
`proc/process.c:259-261`) writes the same buffer to `hal_consolePrint` *and* `posix_write(2,…)`,
both of which land on the UART. Fixed 2:1 ratio across four independent logs (6:3, 6:3, 2:1,
2:1), and in `c1hpa01` another process's output interleaves *between* the two copies.
⚠ The doubling is **EL0-only** — an EL1 dump has no live user stderr and appears once
(`c1pw-on-216762` carries exactly one). My first patch halved both, which by integer division
would have graded that log `KFLT=0` and silently dropped a kernel fault. Caught only by testing
that the column *can* fire.

**Consequence for the C1 hunt:** series #3's fault column is unusable wherever the guard fired,
and `c1hpa01`'s "FAULTS=2" was **one** fault, in ntpclient, caused by me — not two faults in STK.
Neither defect touches the C1 signature itself, which remains open and unexplained.

**3. `GUARD` was counting UART bit-errors** (`2d4a1045`). The three clean trials reported
`GUARD=5/9/6`; **every one of those lines is a corrupted copy of the trace line**, not a guard —
`Cn-hunt:`, `C1 hunt:`, `created a victim-siIe heap`, `c6base = `, and decisively
`0x00F00000091U6000`, where `U` is not a hex digit. This link mangles **~5 of every ~375 malloc
lines (~1.3 %)**, and one bad character defeats the exclusion regex. Two wrong fixes first,
both instructive: filtering the **hex** field changed nothing (corruption lands in the **label** —
`c6base`, `c1sizes`, `c1req 8=` all carry valid 16-digit values), and dropping every line
containing `0x` still left 4/0/4 because corruption mangles the `0x` itself into `0U0`/`hx0`/`0s0`.
Fixed by classifying **positively** on the words real guards use, with the union derived
mechanically from the source; anything unmatched lands in a new **`CORR`** column so a corrupted
*guard* — or a brand-new guard message — shows up as unexplained rather than vanishing.
Verified it still discriminates: **0/0/0** on the clean trials, **142** on the SIG=552 run, **82**
on the SIG=308 run.

⚠ **Evidence-quality note:** at a ~1.3 % line-corruption rate a mangled digit could in principle
fabricate or erase a signature. `SIG` is the resilient selector — it needs an exact
`0x8000000[01]` high half — and is corroborated by `VICT`; but read `CORR` before believing any
single-line claim.

**Series #3 so far (3 of 8):** `SIG=0 VICT=0 GUARD=0 KFLT=0`, ~9 600 frames. C1 is **not**
reproducing in this configuration.

### 🔎 Regression-first on the guard fault — and the anomaly it leaves behind

Applied the owner rule rather than stopping at "my guard is unsafe, fixed it". **0 guard faults
in 878 boots** across 2026-09-18→09-24, then **1 in 3** tonight. Two candidate regressors, both
checked:

- ⛔ **Kernel `ef4500cb` (my `amap_create` edit) — CLEARED.** It is the kernel of the faulting
  run, so it had to be checked. But the diff is genuinely behaviour-neutral (`srcPage + i` is
  identical to the old `*offset + i` after the in-place divide; the closing `*offset = 0` is
  untouched), and **all three trials ran this same kernel while only one faulted**, so it cannot
  be a deterministic cause.
- ⛔ **live[] bookkeeping — UNCHANGED since 09-18.** The only commit touching `malloc_common.live`
  between the guard landing (`5b954c8`, 09-18) and tonight is `999704d` (09-23), a **pure
  addition** of the sweep; it does not alter the ring's insert or clear.

Most likely trigger: **tonight's own allocator churn changed heap layout** (~20 `malloc_dl`
commits, several adding BSS or changing allocation sizes). C1 is already known to be
layout-sensitive, and so, evidently, is this.

⚠ **What this leaves open is more interesting than the guard.** `malloc_liveOverlap` runs on
**every heap allocation**, so it executed millions of times across those 878 boots without ever
faulting — meaning an unmapped entry in `live[]` essentially never occurred before tonight.
The guard is a construction defect fixable by reading (a *plausibility* filter used as a *memory
safety* test), but **an unmapped base reaching `live[]` at all is a real bookkeeping anomaly, and
it is new.** `munmap` is the only return-to-OS in the file and the clear loop is unconditional,
exhaustive and runs before it — so how one got there is unexplained. Hypothesis worth testing,
not yet evidence: the C1-hunt instrumentation itself may be writing into `malloc_common`.
The rebuilt guard now reports `lubase`/`lusize`/`luhlo`/`luhhi`/`luovfl` instead of faulting,
which is the next datum.

#### Host harness now hunts the `live[]` anomaly — and two hypotheses checked off

While the Pi was locked by series #3, moved the open question to the host, where the rate problem
(1 event in 878 boots) is tractable. `tools/malloc-harness/` compiles the **real** `malloc_dl.c`,
so `malloc_common` is directly inspectable.

- **New invariant `HZ_V_LIVE_RING`** (coord `10b9a4b6`): every non-zero `live[]` entry must name a
  region the harness's own mmap/munmap shadow says is mapped, with a matching recorded size, and
  must still be mapped per `msync()`. Both sources are **independent of the bookkeeping under
  test**, so a pass is meaningful rather than circular.
- **Proven it can fail before trusting it**, in all three modes: a slot naming an already-
  `munmap`ed heap (forged from the region table's own tombstone, so the address is one the
  allocator really did map and release — the exact `c1hpa01` signature); a slot holding `0x5000`,
  never an mmap return; and base/size cleared out of step.
- **Result: 200 seeds, 244 763 mmap/munmap cycles, 0 violations.** The ring **logic** is clean on
  the host. ⚠ This cannot reproduce a Phoenix-specific *loss* of a mapping — only a bookkeeping
  bug in the allocator itself — so it narrows the suspect list rather than closing the question.
- 🩹 It also caught that my `malloc_liveOverlap` out-param change had **broken this harness's
  build** (it already had three tests for that function). Fixed, plus two new assertions on the
  out-param.

🤔 **Possible link to C3, recorded but NOT claimed.** C3 is *"two libc globals read back zero…
both live in one 4 KiB `.bss` page"* — **in ntpclient**, the same binary. `malloc_common` is BSS
in that binary and does **span a page boundary** (`0x43b5e0` + `0xce0` crosses `0x43c000`), so a
page-level BSS anomaly would leave part of the struct inconsistent with the rest.
⚠ **But this does not actually explain the observation**: a page replaced by a fresh *zero* page
would zero `live[]`, and a zero entry is *skipped* by the loop rather than dereferenced. So it
would produce silence, not `far=0x5000`. Noted as a direction because the binary and the memory
class coincide, not because the mechanism fits. The rebuilt guard prints `luhlo`/`luhhi`
alongside `lubase`, which is exactly the datum that would discriminate: `heapLo`/`heapHi`
inconsistent with a live entry means page-level corruption, consistent means the slot alone.

⛔ **Checked and NOT supported — do not re-walk:** "an adjacent BSS object overruns into
`malloc_common`". The layout is suggestive — `c1FreeLog` (`0x439dd8`, `0x1800`) ends 8 bytes
below `malloc_common` (`0x43b5e0`) and is C1-hunt instrumentation — but **both** its write
(`c1FreeLogIdx % C1_FREELOG_N`) and its read (`i < C1_FREELOG_N`) are correctly bounded. Also
note `x6`/`x1` in the fault dump are just `&malloc_common` and `+0x10`, i.e. ordinary `live[i]`
iteration — nothing anomalous in the registers.

#### ★ The decisive C1 test no longer needs C1 to fire

Series #3's first five trials exposed a design fault in my own experiment. The mailbox side is
logging perfectly (**24 PAs in every trial**, exactly the bounded cap), but `hpa`/`p4pa` — the
heap physical pages to compare them against — are **0 in all five**, because they print only when
a guard trips. Nothing tripped, so the decisive comparison could not even be attempted.

That is the wrong shape of experiment *here specifically*: the archive says instruments
**suppress** C1 (five in a row drove the rate to 0), so a design that waits on the event is
fighting its own instrumentation.

**Fix:** `capa` logs the **physical page of every heap creation** (libphoenix `9dba4f1`), and
`c1-report` gains a coincidence test (`b299c0a8`). The question becomes one a **clean** run can
answer: *does a mailbox request buffer's physical page ever coincide with a live heap's?* A match
names the mechanism without corruption ever occurring; no match over a large sample is evidence
against it. Confined to the opt-in `C1_HEAP_TRACE_ALL`, never the default build, so it cannot
perturb the rate it studies.

Verified in both directions on synthetic logs before use. ⚠ The no-match wording prints the
sample size and says "weakens **in proportion** to the sample", because `capa` caps at 64/process
and a bare negative from a small sample is exactly what was misread once already
(`V3D_MBOX_LEAKTEST`, n=3).

#### Pre-registered read for the WIDE series (written before it runs)

**Config deliberately unchanged apart from the arm** — same `--profile-laps=2`, same timings as the
15 instrumented runs, so the 10.9 %/trial rate still applies and nothing is confounded with the
offset question. (I considered 4 laps to amortise the ~4 min boot over more exposure — ~25 % more
allocation per wall-clock hour — but it needs `--max-cmd-secs` raised past 760 s or the run is
killed mid-flight, and a mis-sized timeout wastes whole trials. Not worth it for a 25 % gain.)

⚠ **Per-TRIAL guard, not once:** every log must contain the banner
`p4 probe arm = WIDE`. If a trial lacks it, that trial ran the narrow build and its offsets say
nothing — the wide arm is a `LIBC_DIAG` flag, and a `-D` change does not invalidate cached objects,
so a half-applied rebuild is a real failure mode this project has hit before.

**Read:**
- **0 fires in 8** — expected (p = 0.40). Says nothing about offsets, and is *not* evidence the
  wide arm suppresses; that claim was already a coin flip.
- **≥1 fire, ONE offset breaks** — consistent with an offset-specific writer; the genet / xHCI /
  ADMA2 exclusions keep their basis.
- **≥1 fire, SEVERAL offsets break** — ★ the fixed-offset framing is **wrong** and those three
  exclusions **reopen**. This would be the largest change to the suspect list in the hunt.
- Any **`KFLT>0`** is addr2line'd immediately, before anything else.

#### `c1dual` final: 8/8 clean — and it tightens the instrumented-rate evidence

`SIG=0 VICT=0 P4V=0 GUARD=0 KFLT=0` across **8 trials / 27 388 frames**, all reaching teardown.
P(0 in 8) = **0.40** at the measured rate, so this says nothing on its own — as pre-registered.

Its real contribution is to the *other* question: the instrumented build's running tally is now
**2 fires in 15 runs = 13.3 %** against a 10.9 % baseline, P(≥2 in 15) = 0.50. The heavily
instrumented binary keeps firing at **exactly the baseline rate**, which is what refutes "adding
instrumentation silences C1" — and eight more clean trials make that tally, not a suppression
signal.

#### 🔧 The `C1_P4_WIDE` experiment is now BUILDABLE and VERIFIABLE (ready to run)

Three things had to be true before this could be attempted; all three now are.

1. **A way to pass the flag.** `LIBC_DIAG` already exists for exactly this —
   `LIBC_DIAG="-DC1_P4_WIDE" ./scripts/rebuild-rpi4b-fast.sh --scope core …` — and it handles the
   knob-change hazard itself: changing `LIBC_DIAG` **deletes the libphoenix objects**, because a
   `-D` change does not invalidate cached objects. Confirmed `sources/libphoenix/Makefile:45`
   does `CFLAGS += $(LIBC_DIAG)`, so the flag is not silently dropped.
2. **A way to verify it is actually running.** ⚠ Arming the wide probe changes only *numeric
   constants*, so `strings loader.disk` would have had **nothing to match** — the build could not
   be graded, which is precisely what this project forbids. Added a `__attribute__((used))` marker
   (`c1-p4-wide-armed-4-offsets`, libphoenix `c8aa789`) that survives `--gc-sections`. Verified
   both ways on the host harness: **1** occurrence under the arm, **0** by default.
3. **A detector known to work.** Already proven: under the wide arm all four offsets report
   (`p4off = 0x4, 0x404, 0x804, 0xc04`).
4. **A result that cannot be over-read.** ⚠ *"Only +4 broke"* is the expected output **both** when
   the writer is offset-specific **and** when +4 was the only offset armed — opposite conclusions
   from identical text. So the allocator now prints a **one-shot banner** naming the armed offsets
   under the wide arm (libphoenix `9604d7a`; verified 1 under the arm, 0 by default), making the
   **log self-describing** for a reader who no longer has the binary. `c1-report` reads that arm
   **before** the result and, on a narrow build, **refuses to interpret** the offsets at all
   (coord `e05c990b7`). All three paths exercised on synthetic logs.

**Why it is the best next series:** its "suppression" evidence is a **coin flip** (0 in 6 ⇒
p = 0.50 at the measured rate), and it tests the *fixed-offset* premise that the **genet / xHCI /
ADMA2 exclusions were built on**. If the writes turn out not to be offset-specific, those
exclusions reopen — which would be the largest change to the suspect list in the whole hunt.

Run it with the attribution still armed, so a single fire answers **both** questions: where the
write lands, and whether the frame had been a closed BO.

#### ★★★★★ REFUTED: "C1 cannot be studied by adding instrumentation"

`malloc_dl.c` calls this **"the single most important fact about C1"** — that five instrument
changes in a row each silenced it, so *"any change to the binary's write pattern or layout stops it
happening."* It is not supported, and tonight refutes it with a **positive** result rather than
another null.

**1. Every one of the five is a single-arm null against the 4-in-10 baseline the archive does not
contain.** At the measured 10.9 %:

| arm | result | p at measured rate |
|---|---|---|
| `V3D_KEEP_CLOSED_BO` | 0 in 12 | 0.25 |
| closed-BO quarantine | 0 in 5 | 0.56 |
| BO trace | 0 in 7 | 0.45 |
| passive attribution table | 0 in 8 | 0.40 |
| `C1_P4_WIDE` | 0 in 6 | **0.50** |

⚠ Jointly all five showing zero is p ≈ 0.012, which *looks* significant — but each arm is a
**different binary**, and zero-fire arms are the ones that get written up.

**2. The decisive datum: tonight's build is heavily instrumented and fires NORMALLY.** It carries
`capa`, `carel`, `hbopa`, `p4bopa` and a 4 KiB closed-BO table — it changes both the write pattern
and the layout — and across **15 runs it fired twice (13.3 %)** against a 10.9 % baseline
(P(≥2 in 15) = 0.50, i.e. exactly what the baseline predicts). `c1coin` fired `SIG=107`;
`c1pfn1` fired and **halted the kernel**. *(Updated from 2/11 as the `c1dual` series closed; the
conclusion is unchanged — the rate tracks baseline rather than collapsing.)*

➡ **So instrumentation can be added.** That reopens every experiment deferred on this basis,
including the 96 KiB attribution table I held back earlier tonight.

⚠ **Stated precisely:** what is refuted is the strong claim — *any* change suppresses. A specific
heavy instrument might still do so; tonight's is mostly **fire-only** (no UART on a healthy run)
plus 4 KiB. And `V3D_KEEP_CLOSED_BO` keeps its standing, because it has a **paired** arm
(0/12 vs 7/19, Fisher p = 0.019) that assumes no baseline.

➡ **Consequence for `C1_P4_WIDE` specifically:** its suppression evidence is a **coin flip**
(p = 0.50), the detector is **proven capable** (all four offsets report under the wide arm on the
host), and it answers the premise that the genet / xHCI / ADMA2 exclusions were built on —
"the writer hits a FIXED offset". That makes it the best-value next series.

#### ★★★ The kernel victim was a `resource_t`, and kernel memory shares the page pool with BOs

Two questions the kernel halt left open, both now answered by reading:

**1. What was the victim?** `it` was page-aligned, so its containing struct must have its idnode at
offset 0. `thread_t` does **not** (`idlinkage` sits after `next`/`prev`/`locks`/`sleeplinkage`,
~0x38 in), but **`resource_t` and `port_t` both begin with `idnode_t linkage`**. `resource_t` lives
in `process->resources` — precisely the tree the kernel walks when tearing a process down, which is
exactly when the fault fired. So the victim is almost certainly a **`resource_t` at a page-aligned
address**, its `linkage.parent` at offset 0, corrupted high half at **page+4**.

**2. Kernel-owned OR a recycled BO page? — BOTH is possible, and that unifies the readings.**
Kernel heap zones take their pages from **`vm_pageAlloc`** (`vm/zone.c:177`,
`PAGE_OWNER_KERNEL | PAGE_KERNEL_HEAP`), and user mappings hand pages back through
**`vm_pageFree`** (`vm/map.c`) — **the same allocator**. So a frame released by a user `munmap`
(a closed BO's page, say) can be re-issued to a **kernel heap zone** and end up holding a
`resource_t`.

➡ That removes what looked like a contradiction. The device-write model needs no special case for
the kernel victim: a stale physical address is written whenever the frame is recycled, and the
recipient may be user malloc **or** the kernel heap. It also means "the victim was kernel memory"
is *not* evidence against the BO-recycling route — the two are the same pool.

#### ⛔ Checked, NOT a gap — and it proved the multi-offset detector works

Three of the four page-poison probes produce no report, and the harness called that **BLIND**,
which reads as a detector gap in the instrument that underpins "the write lands at page+4".
**It is not a gap.** `c1P4Off[]` is `{4}` by default and `{4, 0x404, 0x804, 0xc04}` only under
`-DC1_P4_WIDE`, because arming all four changes the write pattern enough to stop the bug
reproducing — so on a default build the last three are silent **by design**.

Two fixes so the false alarm is not repeated (coord `eb37b766a`): the probe section now prints the
**armed** offsets and distinguishes an *armed* probe that stayed silent (genuinely blind) from an
*unarmed* one; and `build.sh` takes `MH_CFLAGS`, so compile-time arms can be exercised on the host
rather than only on the Pi.

★ **That immediately produced something useful.** `MH_CFLAGS="-DC1_P4_WIDE"` makes **all four**
report — `p4off = 0x4, 0x404, 0x804, 0xc04`. The multi-offset detector **demonstrably works**, so a
wide-armed run in which only `+4` fires would be genuine evidence that the writer is
**offset-specific**, rather than an artifact of where the probe happens to look. That is the one
remaining way to attack the "page+4 is just where we looked" objection, and it is now known to be
answerable.

#### ▶ Series `c1dual1`–`c1dual8` running — BOTH detectors now attribute (read pre-registered)

Built and verified on the export: `hbopa` (header path) **and** `p4bopa` (poison path) both
present; `rootfs: nfsroot`.

**Read it per FIRE, not per series** — at ~11 %/trial P(≥1 in 8) ≈ 0.61, and every fire tonight has
been informative in a *different* way (mailbox negative, kernel halt, poison-only). On any fire,
record: which detector fired, `hbopa`/`p4bopa`, `hboord`/`p4bord`, and `KFLT`.

⛔ **New standing rule: `KFLT` is now the most important column.** Any `KFLT>0` gets addr2line'd
**immediately, before anything else** — tonight's kernel halt would have been invisible to a
signature-only reading, and it is the single most severe thing C1 has done.

⚠ And `KFLT` itself needs care: an EL1 dump prints **once or twice** depending on whether the
faulting context still has a usable fd 2, so compare `sp`/`fp` across dumps before calling it two
faults. `c1pfn1`'s `KFLT=2` is **one** fault.

#### ★★★★★ C1 HALTED THE KERNEL — and it did it at PAGE+4 of a kernel rbnode

`c1pfn1` carried something no previous run has: **`KFLT=2`**. Every C1 run on record says "the
kernel survives (0 EL1 faults)". **It did not survive this one.**

```
Exception #37: Data Abort (EL1)
 x0=ffffffffc4718000   x1=80000001c4718600   x2=ffffffffc4718000
psr=...345  pc=ffffffffc00268a4  esr=0000000096000004  far=80000001c4718610
```

- `pc` addr2line's to **`lib_idtreeAugment`, kernel `lib/idtree.c:36`** — the ID-tree walk.
- `esr=0x96000004`: bit 6 clear ⇒ a **READ**, level-0 translation fault (I first wrote "write";
  wrong).
- `far` and `x1` are the signature itself: a kernel pointer `0xffffffff_c4718600` whose **high 32
  bits became `0x80000001`**.

★ **And it lands at page+4 — established by DISASSEMBLY, not inference.** The faulting instruction
at `pc` is `ldr x3, [x1, #16]`, followed by `cmp x3, x2`. Reading `lib/idtree.c:36`
(`if (it->parent->right == it)`) against that: **`x1` is `it->parent`** (the corrupted value) and
**`x2` is `it` itself** = `0xffffffffc4718000`, **page-aligned**. `right` is at offset 16, and
`0x…600 + 0x10 = 0x…610 = far` — the arithmetic closes exactly.

`idnode_t` is `{ rbnode_t linkage; int lmaxgap, rmaxgap; int id; }` and `rbnode_t` is
`{ parent; left; right; color }`, so **both `linkage` and `parent` are at offset 0**. The corrupted
word is therefore `it`'s own `parent` field at `it+0`, and its high half is at **`it+4` = page+4**.
The invariant holds in kernel memory as precisely as in a heap header.

↩ **Two corrections to my own first read**, both found by checking rather than asserting:
the faulting line is `it->parent->right`, **not** `p->id`; and `KFLT=2` is **one** kernel fault
reported twice — the two dumps share `sp`/`fp` = `0xffffffffc4305bc0`, so it is one stack, not two
CPUs. ⚠ That also refutes a memory note of mine that an EL1 dump always prints once: it prints
twice when the faulting context still has a usable fd 2.

**Timing:** the fault is at log line 1641, *after* teardown at line 1622
(`Number of frames: 20531`), during process exit — and **zero** further progress lines follow, in
a 1664-line log. The kernel stopped.

➡ **Why this matters more than another heap fire.** No userspace use-after-free can corrupt kernel
memory. A device writing to a **physical** address can, and does not care who owns the frame — so
this is direct confirmation of the device-write model, and it shows the victim frame can belong to
the **kernel** rather than to a recycled malloc heap.

⚠ **Severity correction:** the C1 row has said "crashes STK inside malloc's rbtree; the kernel
survives (0 EL1 faults)". That is no longer true. C1 can **halt the system**, and it did so during
ordinary teardown of a completed run.

⚠ Bounds: the corrupted word's own address is inferred from `x0`/`x2` plus the struct layout, not
printed directly; and two identical EL1 dumps appear, where an EL1 dump is normally emitted once.

✅ **The symbolization was verified, not assumed** — and the check nearly went the other way. The
kernel ELF is dated **2026-09-25 12:55** while the `loader.disk` that booted this run is from
**09-26 03:26**, which looks like symbolizing against a stale binary. It is not: that is the
**only** kernel ELF in the tree, and the build pipeline goes ELF → stripped → image, so the kernel
that ran *is* the one that ELF describes. Confirmed independently by disassembling `amap_create`:
`lsr x19, x2, #12` with **no store back** to the caller's offset is the post-`ef4500cb` form, so
that commit is genuinely in the running kernel (built 12:55, committed 13:24). `git log --since`
shows no other kernel commit. ⚠ Note this also replaces an earlier claim that the run's kernel
"is `ef4500cb`", which had been read off `/etc/build-versions` — the method this project forbids.

#### 🩹 A fire spent for no answer — the attribution was wired to only ONE of the two detectors

`c1pfn1` **fired** (SIG=2) and produced **`hbotot=0`**. Not a void trial, and not the instrument
failing: it fired through the **poison** path — 2 `POISON BROKEN`, 2 distinct `p4page`, and
**zero** corrupt-header reports — while the new physical-frame attribution had been wired only to
the **header** path.

⚠ That is the exact mirror of the mistake the header wiring was created to fix (the old VA-keyed
lookup lived only on the poison path). Hardware surfaced it within two trials, and the cost is
real: at ~11 %/trial a fire is ~1 run in 9, and this one yielded nothing.

**Fixed** (libphoenix `5394af8`): the poison reporter already prints `p4pa`, the physical page, so
it asks the same question with no new machinery — `p4bopa`/`p4botl`, plus `p4bonp`/`p4bord` on a
hit. **Both detectors now ask.**

✅ And the harness now *enforces* it: the page-poison probe section is already a positive control
(it writes `0x80000001` at a page offset and requires the verifier to report it), so it now also
asserts the break reporter consulted the attribution — `poison path asked the BO attribution
1 time(s) -- wired`, and fails loudly if that stops. An attribution wired to one of two detectors
looks perfectly healthy until a fire arrives through the other one.

⏹ Series stopped at 2 trials to rebuild; it could not have answered through the path that was
actually firing.

#### ★★★★★ THE "4-IN-10 BASELINE" IS NOT REAL — and most suppression claims rest on it

Counted every STK-class run in the archive by day (frames ≥ 500, signature-graded):

| day | runs | fired | rate |
|---|---|---|---|
| 09-21 | 5 | 0 | 0 % |
| 09-22 | 12 | 1 | 8 % |
| 09-23 | 87 | 8 | 9 % |
| 09-24 | 99 | 15 | 15 % |
| 09-25 | 64 | 4 | 6 % |
| 09-26 | 9 | 2 | **22 %** |
| **pooled** | **276** | **30** | **10.9 %** |

The **"4-in-10 baseline"** quoted throughout `v3d_phoenix_winsys.c` — and used to justify the
claim that *every* C1 instrument suppresses the event — **is not reproduced by any day in the
archive.** The observed range is 0–22 %.

**What that does to the suppression evidence:**

| claim | p at assumed 4/10 | p at measured **0.11** |
|---|---|---|
| "0 fires in 8 runs" | 0.017 | **0.40** |
| quarantine, "0 in 5" | 0.078 | **0.56** |
| "0 fires in 7 runs" | 0.028 | **0.45** |
| 4 instrumented builds, "0 in 24" | <0.001 | **0.063** |

➡ **None of the single-arm nulls survive.** The belief "adding an instrument silences C1" — which
has shaped this hunt, including tonight's decision to defer the 96 KiB attribution build — rests
on a baseline the archive does not support.

✅ **What still stands, because it never used an assumed baseline:** the paired A/B
`V3D_KEEP_CLOSED_BO=1` **0/12 vs 7/19, Fisher p = 0.019**. A two-arm comparison is immune to this
error, which is precisely why it is the one result to trust.

⚠ **Bound, stated:** 276 runs pool many builds and configurations, so 10.9 % is an average that
represents no single arm. The 4/10 may have been true of one specific stock build. But that cuts
both ways — **if the baseline varies that much by build, then a single-arm null measured against
*any* assumed baseline is unreliable**, which is the methodological point.

➡ **Consequence for tonight:** deferring the hunt build was justified by "it is a known
suppressor". At the measured rate that claim is p = 0.063 — suggestive, not established. The
cheaper 4 KiB physical-frame table now running sidesteps the question anyway, but the deferral
should not be quoted as settled.

#### ▶ Attribution build is LIVE; series `c1pfn1`–`c1pfn8` running (ETA ~05:25)

Built and strings-verified in **both** delivery paths (the check that caught a half-delivered
build earlier tonight): `loader.disk` carries `hbopa` ×19, and the **NFS export** STK carries
`hbopa`, `hbotot` and `capa`. Bench on netboot (`rootfs: nfsroot`).

⚠ **The build log shows `make: *** [supertuxkart] Error 2` — and it is benign.** CMake's STK link
fails on zlib symbols (`deflate`, `deflateEnd`) pulled in via libpng; the script tolerates it
(`+ true`) and the port recipe's **own** link command then relinks STK successfully. Verified the
way that actually settles it — the binary is dated **03:26:46, inside this build**, and contains
the new strings — not by trusting the absence of an error line.

**Pre-registered read** (6 %/trial ⇒ P(≥1 fire in 8) = **0.39**):
- **0/8 — expected, and says nothing** about the attribution or the layout.
- **≥1 fire — the payoff:** `hbopa` answers whether the corrupted frame had been a **closed BO**,
  which is the single measurement separating "recycling is merely necessary" from "recycling is
  the trigger". `hboord` additionally gives the first-ever **close-to-fire latency**, the number
  the quarantine-window objection turns on.
- ⚠ Whatever it says, the answer stands on `hbopa`/`hbotot` together: `hbopa=0` with `hbotot>0`
  means "the driver closed N BOs and this frame was not one of them" — a real answer.
  ↩ **Precision fix:** the reporter is **fire-only**, so `hbotot=0` in a run that did *not* fire is
  **expected and meaningless** (observed on `c1pfn1`). Only `hbotot=0` in a run that **did** fire
  would mean the instrument failed to run, voiding that trial.

✅ **The call site is verified to execute, before spending a rare fire on it.** The host harness
now supplies a fake attribution table and a selftest drives `malloc_reportHeapSize()` directly,
asserting the lookup is called with the **page-aligned physical address**. Checked in **both**
directions: with the call site disabled it reports `NOT CALLED (calls +0)` and 1 detector failure;
restored, `called with the page PA`. Shipping an instrument whose call site had never executed and
then waiting hours for a 1-in-16 event to discover it was never wired is precisely the trap this
project keeps paying for.

#### ★★★★ The discriminator is now obtainable: attribution by PHYSICAL FRAME, in 4 KiB

The blocker was a catch-22 — the measurement that separates the two readings is gated behind a
96 KiB table that is *itself* a known suppressor. Both halves of that turn out to be wrong in a
useful way.

**1. The existing instrument asks the wrong question.** `c1_seen` is keyed on the **CPU virtual
address**. Under the recycling hypothesis that cannot work: when a closed BO's page returns to the
kernel and malloc is handed the frame, **malloc maps it at a different VA**. A VA-keyed table
matches only when the *address* is reused — a separate phenomenon the source already documents
(56 CPU addresses served more than one handle). What survives the kernel's free pool is the
**physical frame**, and the frame is exactly what malloc already prints as `hpa`.

**2. Answering the right question is 24× cheaper.** Storing only what it needs, for **closed** BOs
only — `{first frame, page count, close ordinal}` = **8 bytes** — and BOs are `MAP_CONTIGUOUS`, so
one pair describes a BO exactly. A run closes ~300 BOs, so **512 entries cover a whole run in
4 KiB**, against 96 KiB, with no loss of coverage. The frame is read out of the GPU page table at
close, immediately before those PTEs are invalidated — the last moment the driver knows it.

So it ships in the **default build** (devices `579942b`, libphoenix `4705750`): the binary that
fires is the binary that attributes. On a fire the header reporter now prints `hbopa` (was this
frame a closed BO), `hbotot` (how many closes were recorded), and on a hit `hbonpg`/`hboord`.

➡ `hboord` also bounds the **close-to-fire latency** — the number this project has never had, and
the one the quarantine-window objection to the reframing turns on.

⏹ Series `c1bo` stopped after 3 trials (**0 fires**, as pre-registered: p = 0.69 for 0/3, so it
means nothing) to spend the bench on the discriminator instead. The loop was TERMed so trial 4
never starts while trial 3 finishes cleanly and powers the Pi off.

#### ★★★ REFRAMING: the KEEP_CLOSED_BO result does NOT show that closed-BO pages are the target

This is reasoning from two already-recorded measurements, not a new run — but it changes what the
hunt's strongest "fact" is allowed to claim.

1. If the writer is a **device** writing to a stale **physical** address (which the archive's
   corrupted *kernel* zone-list link argues, since no userspace use-after-free can reach kernel
   memory), then it writes **regardless of who owns the page**. A PA does not care whether the
   driver is holding that page or has released it.
2. The closed-BO **quarantine** held exactly those pages, filled them with `0xa5a5a5a5`, and
   scanned them before release: **~1000 BOs across 5 runs, nothing written**, with the scan proven
   on every run by a deliberately planted C1 word.

➡ If closed-BO pages were the dartboard, (1) says the quarantine should have caught the write, and
(2) says it did not. So **`V3D_KEEP_CLOSED_BO=1`'s suppression (p = 0.019) is better explained by
it changing WHICH physical pages malloc receives** — it withholds ~96 MiB from the kernel pool —
**than by it protecting the pages that get hit.** "Closed BO pages are the victim" is an
interpretation the evidence does not carry; "page recycling through the kernel pool is necessary"
is what both results actually support.

⚠ **Load-bearing PREMISE, stated as one:** step (1) assumes the writer **bypasses the V3D MMU**.
It is true of DMA to RAM (VideoCore, genet, ADMA2) but **false of the V3D core's own writes**,
which go through `W.pt` — and `ioc_close_bo()` zeroes those PTEs on close, so a V3D write to a
closed BO *faults* rather than landing. **If the writer is the V3D core, the quarantine's null is
expected and says nothing about BO pages being the target.** The reframing only bites for a
non-V3D bus master.

⚠ **Bound, and it is currently UNQUANTIFIED.** The quarantine holds 64 slots / 12 MiB, so a BO is
retained for roughly 64 subsequent closes; a write arriving later would be missed. I tried to
bound the close-to-fire latency from the firing run and **cannot**: `bo_hist_record()` writes to an
in-memory ring with no UART output, the quarantine was off, and `V3D_BO_TRACE` is off — the log
carries **no close accounting at all** (verified, not assumed). So this bound stays open.

#### ⚖ The competing reading, which predicts the opposite and is worth more than either assertion

There is a third possibility under which BOTH facts hold **and** closed-BO pages *are* the target:
**the recycling is the TRIGGER, not merely a precondition.** If the write is provoked by the frame
changing hands — a TLB shootdown, a page-table write, or cache maintenance on the
`MAP_UNCACHED`→cacheable transition as the kernel re-issues it — then it explains *all four*
observations at once: `KEEP_CLOSED_BO` suppresses (pages never change hands), the quarantine sees
nothing (pages held, never re-mapped), `memtrip` sees nothing (its pages were never BOs), and the
mailbox is excluded.

➡ **The two readings make opposite predictions and one measurement separates them:** under
"recycling is necessary", the victim PA is ordinary pool churn and *was not* a BO; under
"recycling is the trigger", the victim PA **was** a BO. That is precisely what `hbon`
(libphoenix `39b0c10`) answers — so the hunt build is not merely a nice-to-have, it is the
discriminator between the two live hypotheses.

⚠ **…and its cost is worse than recorded.** `c1_seen` is `void *` + 3 × `uint32_t` = 20 B padded
to **24**, × 4096 = **96 KiB, not the 64 KB written earlier** — which is *exactly* the "passive
96 KB BSS table" this project already measured as suppressing C1. It is very likely the **same
table**. So enabling the hunt build is not a speculative layout risk; it is a change already
observed to silence the event. That is the real reason to hold it, and it makes the discriminator
genuinely hard to obtain.

#### 🔧 Available but not yet spent: the A72 store WATCHPOINT with a value filter

The kernel already exposes a hardware data watchpoint through `platformctl`
(`pctl_watchpoint`, `include/arch/aarch64/generic/generic.h`), built for USB #121 and carrying
exactly the feature C1 wants: **`[trapLo, trapHi)` value filtering** — "the watchpoint halts only
when the value being stored is in range; legitimate stores are emulated and stepped over, keeping
it armed". Arming it on a victim heap's `+4` with the range `0x80000000`–`0x80000002` would halt
on the corrupting store alone and print the **writer's PC**.

⚠ **Two reasons it is not tonight's move, both worth writing down so it is not re-proposed
blindly:**
1. **A hardware watchpoint sees CPU stores only.** If the writer is a device (which the archive's
   corrupted *kernel* zone-list link already argues — no userspace use-after-free can reach kernel
   memory), it will never fire. That silence is itself a clean discriminator, but it is one the
   existing evidence largely gives already.
2. **One comparator** (DBGWVR0/DBGWCR0) against ~95 victim-size heaps per run, and the victim is
   not known in advance — so a single armed page is a ~1-in-95 shot per run, on top of the 6 %
   fire rate.

➡ It becomes the right instrument the moment anything narrows *which* page will be hit — e.g. if
the accumulating `hpa` values from the `c1bo` series show the victim clustering in a predictable
physical band.

#### Pre-registered read of the `c1bo1`–`c1bo6` series (written before it finishes)

At the measured 6 %/trial, P(≥1 fire in 6) = **0.31**. So:

- **0/6 — expected (p = 0.69) and means nothing.** Not evidence of suppression, not evidence of a
  fix, not a reason to change the binary.
- **1/6 — the useful case:** a second `hpa` for the clustering table, which is what decides whether
  the single-comparator watchpoint becomes usable.
- **2+/6 — note it:** that would be above baseline and would suggest the `C1_HEAP_TRACE_ALL`
  layout *raises* the rate, which is worth recording as a lever in its own right.

#### Replication + why the hunt build is NOT being enabled tonight

`c1coin2`, same binary, same command: **SIG=0**, 3351 frames, teardown reached. Replication on
this binary is **1 of 2** — unremarkable either way at the measured 6 %. A 6-trial series
(`c1bo1`–`c1bo6`) is now running on that **same, known-firing** binary to accumulate fires without
touching layout.

⛔ **Deliberately NOT enabling `-DV3D_C1_HUNT` yet, and the reason is quantitative.** The BO
attribution table is `c1_seen[4096]`, and the struct is `void *` + 3 × `uint32_t` = 20 B padded to
**24** → **96 KiB of BSS** (an earlier note here said 64 KB; that was wrong). That is *exactly*
the "passive **96 KB** BSS table" this codebase already measured as suppressing C1 — very likely
the same table, so this is a **known** suppressor rather than a speculative risk. At a 6 % fire rate, a null after enabling it could not be
distinguished from suppression without ~37 trials. So the code is ready and committed, but
spending the night's remaining bench time on a layout change with that risk profile is the wrong
trade against accumulating fires on a binary that demonstrably fires.

⛔ **Ruled out while waiting: BOs are not allocated through the firmware.** Every `mboxProp` call
in the v3d driver is clock / power domain / QPU-enable / firmware-revision — there is **no**
`ALLOCATE_MEMORY` or `LOCK_MEMORY`. So the firmware never learns a BO's physical address, which
removes the most natural way "closed BO page" and "VideoCore response code" could have been the
same mechanism.

#### ➡ Next: ask whether the corrupted page was a CLOSED BUFFER OBJECT

With the in-process mailbox route excluded on a firing run, the best-supported hypothesis left is
the one with an actual measured lever: **`V3D_KEEP_CLOSED_BO=1` gives 0 fires in 12 runs against
7 in 19** (Fisher one-tailed **p = 0.019**, all 31 runs in *one byte-identical binary*). Keeping a
closed BO's pages out of the kernel's free pool suppresses C1 — but *what lands on those pages
after they are recycled has never been identified*.

✅ **The machinery to ask already exists and costs nothing.** `v3d_c1_lookup_page()` answers
"was this page a BO, and had it been closed?" **in-process from a table, with no UART output** —
built that way deliberately, because every noisy instrument suppressed the event (0 fires in 8, 5
and 7 runs against a 4-in-10 baseline).

🩹 **But it was wired only to the poison-break path** — the same mistake `hpa` fixed. Run `c1coin`
produced **107 header fires against 4 breaks**, so the question was being asked almost nowhere.
It is now asked on the corrupt-header path too (libphoenix `39b0c10`), fire-only.

⚠ It is gated behind `-DV3D_C1_HUNT`, which the shipped binary does **not** carry (`p4bon` count
in `c1coin`: 0), so a hunt build is required to use it. Moving the weak declaration ahead of the
new call site was necessary — and the default build **could not see** that breakage, since the
block compiles out. Verified both ways: default CLEAN, `-DV3D_C1_HUNT` CLEAN.

#### ★★★★ C1 FIRED on the coincidence run — and the decisive comparison finally has both sides

Run `c1coin` (2026-09-26 01:19) **reproduced C1** with the new instrumentation armed:
`SIG=107`, **`VICT=1`**, 3158 frames, teardown reached, **`KFLT=0`** (kernel survived).
Victim: **one** heap, VA `0x09ec0000`, `hsize = 0x80000001_0000d000` — the canonical archived
shape, a `0xd000` heap. STK then crashed with `far=0x8000000100000008`: the signature *in the
fault address itself*.

**The comparison the whole hunt was built for, with data on both sides at last:**

| | physical pages |
|---|---|
| corrupted heap header (`hpa`) | `0x8112000` |
| poison-break pages (`p4pa`) | `0x83be000`, `0x842c000`, `0x84e4000`, `0x85d4000` |
| mailbox request buffers (20 distinct) | `0x058a…`, `0x0b0c…`–`0x0e2b…`, `0x2bf0…`, `0xf851…`–`0xface…` |

➡ **NO MATCH — and not even close.** Every corrupted page lies in a tight `0x081…`–`0x085…`
band that touches none of the mailbox clusters. This is the first *direct physical-address*
evidence **against** "the corrupted page was an in-process mailbox request buffer".

⚠ **Read the bound before reading the result — and ↩ I got it wrong twice before checking.** I
wrote "24 calls, all at startup". **False.** By line number in the 2005-line log: rendering starts
at **898**, the 24th (last logged) mailbox call is at **965** — i.e. ~48 % through, *during* the
run — and the first poison break follows at **996**, just **31 lines** later; the first `hpa` fire
at **1247**. So the logged window reaches almost to the first fire, which makes the negative
**stronger** than "startup only", not weaker. What it still cannot cover is mailbox calls *after*
line 965, where the budget ran out — which is why it is raised to 256 (devices `e859b7d`).

⚠ **The four `p4pa` pages are NOT confirmed genuine.** They could be residue from *released* heaps
rather than live corruption. I checked `carel` for them and found nothing — but `carel` hit its
256-line cap at line **803**, before the first break at 996, so **that absence is uninformative**
and the possibility stands. **The mailbox negative therefore rests on `hpa` alone** — which is
sound: it names a live heap's header page and is attested **50:1**.

✅ Second result, independent of the above: **every mailbox page allocated was also released** —
20 distinct pages, 20 releases, empty set difference. First *runtime* corroboration of the
nine-site audit, which previously rested on a leak counter alone. Again bounded to startup.

★ **Method note — majority vote beat the bit-errors.** At the measured ~1.3 % UART corruption
rate the raw distinct-value lists were misleading: `hpa` showed two values and `hsize` two.
Frequency settles both instantly — **50:1** for `hpa = 0x8112000` and **49:1** for the canonical
`hsize`. Quoting `sort -u` output here would have invented a second physical page that does not
exist. Use frequency, not distinctness, on a lossy link.

★★ **UPGRADE — the logging bound does not actually limit the conclusion.** The driver's
deliberate-leak message (`timed out AFTER the doorbell … leaking its message page on purpose`) is
**unbounded** — no counter, printed on every timeout. It fired **0 times** in this run, so
**no mailbox call timed out anywhere in the run**, not merely among the 24 logged ones. A page is
only ever returned to the pool with the firmware still owning it on that timeout path; with zero
timeouts, no mailbox page can have been left stale — *including the unlogged calls after line 965*.

So three strands agree, one of them unbounded:

| strand | coverage | result |
|---|---|---|
| timeout / deliberate-leak detector | **whole run (unbounded)** | **0 leaks** |
| buffer released after its response surfaced | 24 logged calls | 24/24, 20 distinct pages |
| corrupted page PA vs mailbox page PAs | 24 logged calls | **no match** |

➡ **The in-process v3d mailbox path is excluded as the cause of THIS run's C1 event** — and for
the first time that is established on a **firing** run, where previously it rested on a leak
counter read during clean ones.

⛔ Also ruled out by address this run: the **framebuffer** (`0x3d3b2000`–`0x3db9b000`, HVS
DMA-writes it continuously) and the **vcmbox persistent buffer** (`0x03d3f000`) — the corrupted
band `0x0811…`–`0x085d…` overlaps neither.

⚠ **Keep the two negatives apart.** The `capa` "no coincidence" covers only the **first 64 heaps**
(`caCap`, not the 512-line budget, is the binding limit) and the victim heap `0x09ec0000` is almost
certainly *not* in that sample — so it is a much weaker claim than the `hpa` result and must not be
quoted alongside it as one finding.

★ **The crash is a DIFFERENT consumer than the archive records.** `pc=0x16d9b58` addr2line's to
**`_mesa_delete_vao`**, not `lib_rbRemoveBalance` — a Mesa object pointer whose high half became
`0x80000001`, dereferenced at +8 during teardown (`esr=0x92000004` = level-0 translation fault, the
high bits having made the address non-canonical). So the corrupted word reaches at least two
unrelated consumers, matching the archived "Mesa `hash_table::mem_ctx`" site.

`NOT MAPPED` fired **0** times, so the `live[]` anomaly did not recur this run.

#### ⚠ Trap: a `setsid` cycle reports "completed" while it is still running

Launched the coincidence cycle as `setsid … &` with a 600 s tool timeout. The timeout killed only
the **wrapper**; the cycle kept driving the Pi. The task notification arrived minutes early saying
*completed, rc=0*, with a **0-byte UART log and every counter at 0**.

That reads exactly like a void run — and the obvious response, re-running it, would have started a
**second concurrent cycle and killed the real one in flight** (one UART). `pgrep` showed PIDs
4030118/4030261/4030264 alive and the log mtime 34 s old: not void, just started.

**Rule adopted:** after any cycle notification, `pgrep -a -f 'psh-interact|picocom|test-cycle'`
*before* concluding anything, and wait on the real PID rather than the task. An empty log with a
fresh mtime is a **running** cycle, never a failed one.

#### ✅ HW results: va2pa suite 5/5, and a silent-zero trap caught before it fired

**⚠ The build was only HALF delivered, and strings-verification caught it.** `--scope core`
produced a `loader.disk` carrying both new strings (19 each) — but the **NFS export binaries had
neither** (`capa`: 0, `RELEASED`: 0, `NOT MAPPED`: 0). Netboot serves the kernel from TFTP and the
rootfs from a *hand-maintained* NFS export, and `--scope core` does not sync it. Had I run the
coincidence experiment then, STK would have emitted **no `capa` lines at all** and I would have
read that as *"no coincidence"* — a textbook silent zero. Fixed with `sync-netboot-tree.sh`; all
strings re-verified present on the export afterwards.

**Cycle 1 — `libc/misc/va2pa`: 5/5 PASS on hardware**, each as its own tagged line,
`5 Tests 0 Failures 0 Ignored / OK`, 0 fault patterns. Notably `unmapped_unaligned_…` passes, so
`va2pa` really does return the in-page **offset** on an unmapped unaligned address — the guard's
page-alignment precondition is load-bearing, not theoretical. `null_resolves_to_zero` passing
confirms the evidence-based call on page 0.
↩ *On "check it can fail":* there is no fix to stash for a contract test, so the analogous check is
whether the assertions discriminate — **no constant return value can satisfy all five**, since one
case demands `0` on a dead page and another demands `64` on that *same* dead page.

**Cycle 2 — ntpclient with `C1_HEAP_TRACE_ALL=1`:** it creates **exactly one heap** — VA `0x2000`,
size `0x1000`, PA `0x2f6bc000`. 0 NOT-MAPPED reports, 0 releases, 0 faults, so the guard fix runs
clean. ⚠ **This does NOT settle corrupt-slot vs honest-heap for the `0x5000` entry**: `0x2000 +
0x1000 = 0x3000`, and a few more allocations would put a heap at `0x5000` legitimately. It shows
only that the nominal run never gets there.

#### Series #3 final: 8/8 clean — a null at this layout, NOT a negative

`SIG=0 VICT=0 GUARD=0 KFLT=0` across **8 trials / 27 424 frames**, all reaching teardown. At the
measured 6.1 % rate **P(0 fires in 8) = 0.60**, so this is exactly what a working bug looks like
when sampled eight times. It says nothing about any change made tonight.

It also produced no mailbox correlation, for the structural reason recorded below: `hpa`/`p4pa`
are fire-gated, so a clean series cannot feed them. That is the gap `capa` now closes.

#### ★★ MEASURED: C1's fire rate is ~6 %, not "1 in 3" — fire-gated sampling is infeasible

Counted every STK-class trial in tonight's archive (frames ≥ 500) rather than reasoning from the
figure on record:

| | |
|---|---|
| STK-class trials, 2026-09-25/26 | **66** |
| trials that fired (`SIG` > 0) | **4** — `c1trace-armed` 52, `c1gc` 308, `c1pa1`@22:22 552, +1 |
| per-trial rate | **6.1 % (1 in 16.5)** |
| P(0 fires in the 14 trials since the last one) | **0.42** |
| trials for a 90 % chance of ≥ 1 fire | **37** ≈ **8.6 h of bench time** |

Two conclusions, both load-bearing:

1. ⛔ **The "~1-in-3" figure this hunt has been navigating by is ~5× optimistic.** ↩ To be fair to
   the docs: the KNOWN-ISSUES C1 row **already recorded 4.9 %** (STK 27/552) and this count agrees
   with it — the bad number lived in working notes and in my own planning, not in the row. Every
   power calculation made against 1-in-3 was wrong, including my reading of the underpowered A/Bs.
   (Caveat: the 66 trials pool several builds and configs, so this is a pooled overnight rate.)
2. ⛔ **The 14-trial drought since the last fire is NOT suppression.** At 6.1 % its probability is
   **0.42** — entirely unremarkable. So `f2d47c1` (and series #3's build generally) is **not**
   implicated, and I am not going to go hunting a regression that the arithmetic says isn't there.

➡ This is the quantitative justification for the pivot below: at ~8.6 h per expected fire,
**any experiment that waits on C1 firing cannot be run overnight**. The coincidence test is the
one that does not have to.

#### ★ The coincidence test now targets the ONE untested mailbox route

Re-read the refuted mailbox hypothesis properly rather than treating "REFUTED" as closed. The
refutation is **specific to release-by-`munmap`** (the leak counter reads 0; all nine in-tree
mailbox sites are now structurally safe), and that memory names the survivor outright:

> *"The one release path no leak counter can observe is still untested: **process exit while a
> property call is in flight** — the kernel reclaims every page with no `munmap` and no counter."*

Measured on a series log: the in-process driver allocates a **fresh page per mailbox call** —
**19 distinct PAs across 24 logged calls** — so those frames return to the pool and malloc reuses
them constantly. A raw PA match would therefore have been **dominated by benign recycling**, and
the report would have announced it as the mechanism. The mailbox PAs also sit in the same region
as the archived C1 victim heaps (`0x09f99000`, `0x0b5e2000` vs `0x0b59a000`, `0x09131000`).

So the driver now logs the **release** as well as the allocation (devices `62cbcb5`), and the
report classifies each coincidence (coord `545a29ac`+):
- a mailbox page **with** a `RELEASED` line — the firmware had finished, harmless recycling;
- a mailbox page with **no** release line — ⚠ the firmware may still own it. **That is the
  untested route's signature.**

🩹 While there: fixed a **latent `-Werror` break** in the same file — `v3d_phoenix_powerOn()` is
defined at the bottom and called from `v3d_phoenix_reset()` hundreds of lines above with no
declaration, which gcc-16 rejects as an implicit declaration. Confirmed **pre-existing** by
compiling HEAD with my change removed (same error) before blaming my own edit.

#### Pre-registered: how the next run gets read (written BEFORE running it)

Tonight has produced seven wrong selectors, so the interpretation rules go down first.

**Order (after `SERIES 3 COMPLETE`, Pi is free) — one build, then three cycles:**
1. `--scope core` rebuild, then strings-verify **both** new strings are in the image —
   `ring says live, pmap says gone` **and** `capa   =`. Never `/etc/build-versions`.
2. **Cycle 1 — the new test suite.** `/bin/test-libc-misc -v -g va2pa`, graded by **five**
   individual `TEST(va2pa, …)` PASS lines. A red test here is a regression I shipped, and it is
   fixed before anything else proceeds.
3. **Cycle 2 — ntpclient**, `C1_HEAP_TRACE_ALL=1` as **one** `env` command (the gate is
   per-process, and `--idle-secs` is per-command, so a bare `export` burns the window).
   `--idle-secs 60` is plenty; it is a tiny program. Read `cabase` for `0x5000`, and `lubase` if
   the NOT-MAPPED report fires.
4. **Cycle 3 — STK** with `C1_HEAP_TRACE_ALL=1`: the coincidence run.

**Reading rules, fixed in advance:**
- ⛔ **A run where the `NOT MAPPED` report never fires is NOT a negative.** I added ~2 KiB to
  `malloc_common`, and this codebase's own record is that instrumented builds went **0/24** where
  stock was ~1-in-3. The prior rate is **1/3** (one fault in three trials), so 8 boots give ~96 %
  chance of ≥1 fire *only if the rate held*. Silence means "the instrument is unproven at this
  layout", never "fixed".
- The report must be seen **firing at least once** before any conclusion rests on it.
- `lusize` is **evidence, not proof**: nonsense ⇒ corrupt slot, but a legal page-multiple is
  consistent with *both* readings (an overwrite of an occupied slot leaves the old occupant's
  size). Only `cabase` from the all-trace settles whether `0x5000` was ever an mmap return.
- Grade faults by **KFLT/UFLT**, never by raw `Exception #` lines, and check **CORR** before
  believing any single-line claim.

### ★★★★★ C1 unified: a 32-bit `0x8000000x` store at PAGE+4

↩ Earlier tonight I "refuted" the page+4 reading because the `chunk->heap` fire landed at
`chunk+12`. **That was my error** — I never checked where `chunk+12` falls. `CHUNK_OVERHEAD` is 16
and `ptr = chunk + CHUNK_OVERHEAD`, so `ptr=0x0c9ce008` → `chunk=0x0c9cdff8` →
`&chunk->heap = 0x0c9ce000` (page-aligned) → high half at **`0x0c9ce004` = page+4**.

★★ **Three detection paths agree, and two are independent of the poison instrument** — which is
precisely what the archived warning ("page+4 is where the instrument looks") was about:

| # | path | why it lands on page+4 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | `heap->size` high half | at `heap+4`; heaps are mmap'd page-aligned |
| 2 | `chunk->heap` high half | `chunk` at page−8 ⇒ field at page, high half at page+4 |
| 3 | page-poison break | reported directly as `p4off = 0x4` |

➕ **Model:** the writer stores a 32-bit `0x80000000`/`0x80000001` at **page+4 of many pages**, and
is only *noticed* where page+4 happens to hold something validated. That also explains why the
`chunk->heap` signature is rare: only a chunk at page−8 puts its `heap` field's high half there.

★ Method note: an offset means nothing without its base. `chunk+12` and `page+4` looked like
competing claims and were the same address.

### ★★★★★ A heavy fire (SIG=552) — and it exposed a gap in my own instrumentation

Series #2 trial 1 (`c1pa1` @22:22, STK `7704a8846a8df148`) fired hard:

| SIG | corrupt-header fires | `hfixed=1` | `hhi32=0x80000000` / `0x80000001` | frames | TDOWN | EL1 faults |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **552** | 139 | 273 | **117 / 156** | 3461 | yes | 0 |

`hlo32 = 0xd000` on 270 of them, and `caller` resolves to `operator delete`. Both corrupting values
appear in one run, roughly balanced — consistent with the two mailbox response codes rather than
with any single marker.

⚠ **But the decisive comparison could not run.** That run produced **zero poison breaks**, and
`p4pa` only prints on a break — so `corrupted page PAs: none`. The mailbox side worked perfectly: 19
distinct in-process request pages logged (`0x04f8d000` reused four times), with vcmbox's persistent
`buf_pa=0x03ddd000` correctly shown as the reference that must never match.

➡ **The header path is where the events are** (139 fires vs 0 breaks), so keying the experiment on
the rarer signature was my error. Fixed: `malloc_reportHeapSize` now prints **`hpa`**, the heap's
physical page (libphoenix `f2d47c1`), and `c1-report.sh` correlates on either. Proven both ways —
and the first regex matched nothing because the field has three spaces before `=`, not four, which
only testing caught.

⚠ Two label collisions tonight (`c1s`, then `c1pa1`) each caused a misread, because `ls | head -1`
sorts **oldest** first. Name runs uniquely; grade with `ls -t`.

### C1 sampling series #1 — 6/6 clean, and the tooling that now grades a fire instantly

| trials | SIG | frames each | TDOWN | faults | heaps traced |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 6 | **0** | 3332–3467 | 6/6 yes | 0 | 94–96 |

A properly-exposed negative, not a surprise: at the measured ~7–10 % per run, P(0 in 6) ≈ 0.53–0.65.
Every trial completed *and* tore down, which is where detection is amplified, and each traced ~95
heaps inside the window holding all 25 archived victims.

**What changed while they ran** — a firing run now grades itself:

- `c1-report.sh` correlates a broken page's **physical** address (`p4pa`) against the in-process
  mailbox request buffers, printing either `*** MATCH … WAS a mailbox request buffer` or a plain
  no-match. Proven both ways on synthetic logs. `rpi4-vcmbox`'s persistent buffer
  (`buf_pa=0x03dc2000`) is printed as the reference that must **never** match.
- `v3d_phoenix_power.c` logs each request buffer's physical page (bounded, devices `ac44e5d`) —
  that is the path that matters, since vcmbox serialises through one buffer it never frees while
  this one mmaps/munmaps a fresh page per call, reached from `v3d_phoenix_powerOn()`.

⚠ **And the honest tension:** `V3D_MBOX_LEAKTEST` forces exactly this mechanism (page returned to
the kernel *before* the doorbell) and measured **0/3 vs 1/3** — no effect. But n=3 cannot show an
effect at a few-%-per-run rate, so it is **underpowered, not negative**, and must not be quoted as a
refutation. `p4pa` settles it without needing either argument.

### C1 — sampling plan (the hunt is now a numbers game)

Measured rate is a few % per run (exposure-matched ~7–15%), and the SIG=308 run was a heavy
outlier — `c1wide` right after it was completely clean. So catching the victim heap's *creation*
needs several runs on **one** build, not more rebuilds.

- **Build**: trace heaps overlapping `[0x09000000, 0x11000000)` — the window holding **all 25**
  archived victim pages — budget 96. Replaces the count budget that provably stopped 43 MB short.
- **Run loop**: back-to-back cycles, no rebuild between. Each is ~25 min, of which ~12 min is wasted
  because `--idle-secs` is a per-command timeout and the `export` burns a whole window.
- **Free speed-up to try first**: `/usr/bin/env C1_HEAP_TRACE=1 stk …` as a *single* command
  (confirmed `/usr/bin/env` exists on the export). Zero build cost, and it self-verifies — if
  `C1-hunt: created` is absent the env did not propagate and I fall back to two commands.

⚠ Grading per run must check **three** things, each of which has already gone wrong once:
`SIG` (signature, not guard name), `TDOWN` (the exit free-storm is where detection happens), and
`LIVE` (the ralloc control is **not** exercised every run).

### C1 — the night's hunt (detail moved out)

➡ **Full narrative: [`docs/misc/2026-09-25-c1-hunt-night.md`](../misc/2026-09-25-c1-hunt-night.md)**
— ~317 lines, moved so this log stays readable. Live status is always the
[C1 row in KNOWN-ISSUES](../KNOWN-ISSUES.md).

★★★★★ **Armed run `c1gc` — three results, one of them refuting my own inference.**
SIG=308, 3424 frames, teardown reached.

1. ✅ **The ralloc `get_gc_block_header` hypothesis is ELIMINATED.** The positive control proves the
   function *is* reached, C1 was rampant in the same run, and the plausibility check fired **0**
   times. Bounded only by what that check can detect.
2. ↩ **The victim is NOT a whole `0x5000` heap.** `0xd000` (42/48) and `0x5000` (6/8) were traced
   **exhaustively with budgets unexhausted**, and **0 breaks fell inside any traced heap**. So the
   `0x4ff0` chunk is a **fragment of a larger, untraced heap**, not the whole of a `0x5000` one —
   and the 61/61 `p4csize` agreement means less than it appeared.
3. ★ **C1 demonstrably crashes the application, in malloc's own bin tree.** EL0 Data Abort at
   **`lib_rbRemoveBalance` (`libphoenix/sys/rb.c:148`)**, `far=0x8` — the red-black tree used for
   **large bins**. ✅ Kernel survived: 0 EL1 faults, psh alive after.

➡ Next: trace the remaining legal heap sizes (`0x3000 0x4000 0x7000 0x9000 0x11000 0x19000
0x21000`) to catch the victim's creation and name its allocator.

**Where C1 actually stands after tonight:**

- **Open and ACTIVE.** Latest occurrence 2026-09-25 09:14 in a **quake3** run. By signature,
  24/793 GPU-bearing runs before 2026-09-24 16:00 and 4/65 since — no quiet period.
  Per app: STK 27/552 (4.9%), quake3 1/84, and **0** in quake2/quakespasm/vkquake.
- **The armed run reproduced it** (SIG=52) *with* the trace on, so this instrument does not
  suppress the event. Per-size allocators: `0xd000` ← Mesa ralloc `gc_zalloc_size`;
  `0x5000` ← `operator new`. ⚠ `c1call` resolves to `operator new` for C++ paths — a wall.
- **The page-poison guard is a direct observation of the writer**: 61/61 breaks agree on
  `p4got=0x80000001`, `p4csize=0x4ff0`, chunk page-aligned+0x10 — a whole `0x5000` heap.
  But `0x5000` is a whole bin (any 12–16 KB allocation), so that agreement may be frequency,
  not selection.
- **Refuted from the archive:** "always a `0xd000` heap" (a `0x2000` victim is on record) and
  "always page+4" (the latest fire corrupts a `chunk->heap` pointer at `chunk+12`). The real
  invariant is: **high 32 bits ← `0x80000000`/`0x80000001`, low half intact.**
- **`V3D_KEEP_CLOSED_BO=1` still suppresses** (0/20 by signature) — it survives the recount.
- ⛔ **Do not re-walk the ralloc lead.** The allocator was already known, the
  `flags = IS_PADDING` value theory is already retracted, and the designed plausibility check in
  `get_gc_block_header()` already ships. Its never-firing is **not yet evidence** — a positive
  control was added to tell "always sane" from "never reached".

**Four of my own measurements were wrong first**, each from a too-narrow selector: a `*stk*.log`
glob, then `why =` alone, then a guard-name list, then a five-message subset. The fix that
generalises: select on the **property** (the signature), not on how the producer worded it.

### P3 narrowed (reading only, gate NOT lifted)

The row says lifting the `dc zva` gate needs "no caller of `hal_memset` can pass uncached memory".
But the failure the row rests on is a hang in `_log_init`'s first large zeroing, and that call is
`hal_memset(&log_common, 0, sizeof(log_common))` where `log_common` is a file-scope struct in
ordinary kernel `.bss` — Normal **cacheable**, not Device, not Normal-NC. So "uncached memory" does
not explain *that* hang; what is left is the window right after the D-cache is enabled.

⛔ Observation from reading, not a reproduction. The whole-kernel argument over all 108 call sites
(39 posix / 30 hal / 23 proc / 6 test / 4 vm / 3 log / 2 lib / 1 syscalls) is still owed and the
item stays perf-only with correctness risk. The value is that the next person should re-test the
`_log_init` window rather than audit callers for uncached memory.

### P5 re-scoped — its stated fix does not exist, and the replacement is contraindicated

I claimed last turn that P5's "clean fix is a plo boot-order gate" is wrong. Verified it rather than
leaving the claim standing:

- plo's `app` lines only **register** programs. `main.c:83-120` then walks the syspage list calling
  `proc_syspageSpawn` back to back with **no wait between entries** — `app` order is spawn order,
  nothing more. `cmds/wait.c` runs before `go!`, i.e. before any program exists, so it cannot gate.
- A real gate *is* constructible: `-x` sets `flagSyspageExec` (`plo/cmds/app.c:199`) → the `X` the
  kernel tests, so dropping `-x` loads psh without running it, and the takeover could start it by
  name with `spawnSyspage()` (exposed in `sys/threads.h:60`).
- ⛔ **Not doing that.** It makes the shell conditional on the takeover, and the `/etc/rc.nfsroot`
  (`psh -i`) experiment already measured that shape: no-prompt boots went **0 of 134 → 5 of 88**.
  A boot with no shell costs far more than this race. Row now says so, so nobody re-attempts either.

Owner decision, not an autonomous change: accept the race, or gate psh and accept no shell on a
failed takeover.

### Kernel hardening: `amap_create`'s scratch no longer borrows the caller's variable

Not a bug fix — a latent trap closed. `amap_create` divided the caller's `*offset` in place to index
the source amap in pages, restoring it to 0 only at the very end. Correct today (both early returns
sit above the division), but the rest of the amap API is **byte**-based, so any early return added
between those two points would silently corrupt every later `amap_*` index for that entry. The
scratch is now a local (kernel `ef4500cb`).

Found while reviewing `amap_create` as a C3 suspect; it was cleared as the cause, and this is only
the fragility that review turned up. **C3 is unchanged and still waiting on a recurrence.**

Verified: `-Werror` clean; 3/3 netboots, 0 faults; 188 libc tests (exit/pthread/signal/stdio), 0
failures; `test-exec` 1–5 exec `/bin/to_exec` with the expected argv/environ, 0 faults.

### ✅ D9 CLOSED — a stock boot log no longer opens with 20 opaque characters

Every boot opened with `1111444422225555AS0` + `TR0`–`TR3` + a late `A1` — armstub milestones from
each of 4 cores, the reloc trampoline's per-core marker, and plo's exit-EL digit. First impression
of a published tree, and unreadable without the decoder table.

Now gated behind **`RPI4_EARLY_MARKERS`** in `board_config.h`, **default 0**. A stock log opens with
`hal: console_init done`.

**Gated, not deleted** — I checked first whether anything keys on them, and it does:
`summarize-rpi4b-uart-log.py` uses `AS0` vs `TR0..TR3` to tell a firmware-handoff failure from a
trampoline one, and when a boot hangs before the console exists they are the *only* evidence. They
stay single characters because four cores share one unlocked polled UART — strings would interleave.

One source of truth: plo reads the macro directly; the armstub and trampoline are assembled
standalone with no `board_config.h` on their include path, so `build.project` greps it and passes
`-DRPI4_EARLY_MARKERS` — the mechanism `RPI4_LOG_TO_FILE` already uses.

**Proven both ways**, by disassembly count of the macro's TXFF spin and then on hardware:

| component | gate off | gate on | matches source |
|---|---|---|---|
| armstub | 0 | 9 | 4 markers + `AS0\r\n` ✓ |
| kernel8-reloc | 0 | 20 | 4 cores × 5 chars ✓ |
| plo | 92 | 100 | the 8 `hal_exitToEL1` calls ✓ (92 = `_exc_dump`, never gated) |

Markers-on boot reproduces `1111444422225555AS0`/`TR0`–`TR3`/`A1` exactly. Markers-off is **3/3
trials** to psh, 0 of those literals, 0 faults, 4-core evidence (`smp release-2`, `r0p3 x4`) intact
— benched rather than single-booted because removing the FIFO-full spins changes secondary-core
timing.

⚠ **Trap found and documented in the header**: editing `board_config.h` does **not** invalidate
plo's cached objects. My first markers-on build had the armstub and TR markers back but plo's `A`
still missing — a half-applied gate that graded green. Flipping the macro needs a touch of plo's
`_init.S`. This is `board_config.h` generally, not this macro (`PLO_SMP_ENABLE` too).

Also fixed `uart-summary.sh`'s `132AS0|^132` armstub pattern, which never matched the real 4-core
output, and added a trampoline stage. The summarizer's new "markers are compiled out" hint was
checked to actually fire on a synthetic early-failure log, not just to stay quiet on a healthy one.

`scripts/check-early-markers.sh` (new, allowlisted) grades all three components against the header
and names the half-applied case — proven red by pointing it at a flipped header with unrebuilt
binaries.

*Reclassified:* my earlier notes filed D9 under "owner-gated". Re-reading the row, "worth doing in
one supervised pass" was my own caution about not splitting it across turns — not an owner gate
(C6 says "owner-gated" explicitly; D9 does not). Done in one pass accordingly.

### ✅ D4 CLOSED — plo now dumps registers on a bootloader exception (plo `b5bfbea`)

plo's 16 exception vector slots each printed **one letter** and halted. The real dispatcher existed
but hardcodes `esr_el3`/`elr_el3`/…, while that single table is installed at **EL3, EL2 and EL1** —
so at EL1 the dumper itself trapped. That is why the letters were there.

Fix: the slots share a `CurrentEL`-aware `_exc_dump` that reads the faulting EL's own bank and
prints `ESR/ELR/FAR/SPSR/SCTLR`, keeping the slot tag.

**Graded by making it fire, not by a clean boot** — three throwaway builds, each predicted from the
disassembly before booting:

| `brk #0` placed after | result | ELR vs. `brk` address |
|---|---|---|
| `msr vbar_el1, x0` | `EXCE EL1  ESR=f2000000  SPR=…3c5` | `002001a8` = exact ✓ |
| `msr vbar_el2, x0` | `EXCE EL2  ESR=f2000000  SPR=…3c9` | `00200170` = exact ✓ |
| `msr vbar_el3, x0` | **no dump — booted clean to psh** | `start_el3` never runs |

Slot `E` = index 4 = offset 0x200 (current EL, SP_ELx, sync); `ESR` EC `0x3C` = BRK. The EL1 and EL2
runs read *different* register banks and returned different correct ELRs, so the dispatch genuinely
selects rather than falling through.

⚠ **The EL3 arm stays unproven** — the third boot shows the Pi 4 firmware hands plo control at EL2,
so `start_el3` is unreachable on this board. It differs from the two proven arms only in register
names, and the docs say so rather than claiming three-for-three.

Reverted and re-verified: 0 `brk` in `plo.elf`, 16 slots at exact 0x80 spacing, clean boot with the
loader self-reporting `plo b5bfbeadc124` (no `+dirty`), 0 faults, 0 `EXC` dumps.

Diagnosability only: plo takes no exception on a healthy boot, and the loader is re-fetched over
TFTP every boot, so recovery is free.

## 0. ★★★★★ NIGHT OF 2026-09-24/25 — TEN libc defect clusters, all gated and pushed

One screen for the owner. Full detail is in
[`docs/misc/2026-09-25-libc-defect-cluster.md`](../misc/2026-09-25-libc-defect-cluster.md), whose
lettered sections run in the order things happened (0z first, 08 last).

| § | area | defect | user-visible effect |
|---|---|---|---|
| 0z | `setvbuf` | buffer dropped on an equal-size request | **every `setlinebuf()` stream ran UNBUFFERED**; bash `echo` on ext2 **32 s → 0 s** |
| 0y | `freopen` | error/EOF indicators never cleared | a reopened file **read as empty**, data unreachable |
| 0v | `strtol`/`strtoul` | `endptr` set to `nptr` on overflow | parsing a list of numbers **stalls** on an out-of-range one |
| 0v | `strtoul`/`strtoull` | `-LONG_MIN` signed-overflow UB | latent; warns on host, silent on target |
| 0t | `strtod` | subnormals with many digits flushed to zero | **any double round-tripped via `%.17g` became 0** |
| 0s | `printf` | exact ties rounded away from zero | `%.0f` of 0.5 → `1`; `%.2f` of 0.125 → `0.13` |
| 0r | `strftime` | **8** signed year/epoch conversions via `%u` | pre-1970 / pre-1900 dates → 20-digit garbage |
| 0q | `scanf` | suppressed conversions mis-accounted | `%n` short, `0x` unseen, **OOB read of `buf[-1]`** |
| 0o | `scanf` | incomplete float items accepted | `sscanf("1e","%lf")` returned 1 — malformed input accepted |
| — | 4 files | missing includes | invisible to the target build under `-Werror` |

**Every fix proven RED before GREEN.** Verified by the full libc suites (printf 134, stdio 95, stdlib
95, time 44, scanf 35+48, plus 455 in stdio-adjacent suites) and **THREE 6/6 showcase gates, 0 faults
each**, frames *looked at* rather than logged (X desktop composited; STK a fully-lit race with HUD;
Q2 textured 3D at 38.8 fps, not its historical black; vkQuake with both archway torches lit). Torch
ROI PRESENT on all three.

**Six host harnesses** now run libphoenix's REAL source against glibc in ~1 s each, no hardware:
`tools/lib{string,wchar,num,fmt,time,scanf}-hosttest/`, all runnable via
`./scripts/run-libc-hosttests.sh`. Three of the ten clusters were found on the host before any Pi
cycle.

**Beyond libc, same night** (sections 0o–0c): **D6 CLOSED** (`vkCmdSetDepthBias` calls the real
implementation; the tracked "populate V3DV's dispatch" fix proved unnecessary). **P3/TD-20's EL2
question ANSWERED** by `tools/dczid-probe/` — `DZP=0`, no trap — **and the hypothesis was wrong**, so
the gate stays for a better-supported reason. **G1 re-measured**: 14 fps, not 10. **D7** lists a
deviation with no marker in source, and its framing is off-policy under fork-only. **D8**'s "unused
WHD" is now a measured fact (1.6 MB / 75 files, `LWIP_WIFI_BUILD=no` everywhere). **C1**: 16 solid STK
runs at 0 fires with the detector proven armed, a clean before/after boundary — and **my own
correlation hypothesis refuted by disassembly** (STK has 0 `setvbuf` call sites).

⚖ **Three deliberate NON-fixes, each with its evidence** — the parts most worth the owner's review:
1. **I reverted my own fix** to printf's scientific-form tie: it passed every string comparison and
   silently broke `%.17g` round-tripping for 3 doubles in 400 000. Documented in `format.c`.
2. **scanf's 84 EOF-vs-0 return differences left alone** — whether a *suppressed* conversion
   "completes" is genuinely ambiguous in C17 and our reading of `%*d` is arguably the more literal
   one; changing scanf's return values on a contested reading risks every port.
3. **glibc's C23 `0b` prefix** not adopted — an extension C17 does not require.

★★ **The transferable lesson: closed identities beat reference comparison.** `%.17g` round-tripping
and `timegm(gmtime_r(t)) == t` need no glibc to be authoritative — arithmetic is the judge. That is
what caught the bad fix in (1). Second: **compiling target-only code with a second compiler is itself
a diagnostic** — four missing-include/type defects fell out before a single test ran.


➡ **Full detail for the night** — every defect with its gate, the three showcase gates, the six
host harnesses, and the issue-list audit (C1 / C4 / C5 / C8 / G1 / G3 / D6 / D7 / D8 / P3 / C2) —
lives in [`docs/misc/2026-09-25-libc-defect-cluster.md`](../misc/2026-09-25-libc-defect-cluster.md).

## 0. WHERE THINGS STAND — 2026-09-23 (overnight)

**The open list is [`docs/KNOWN-ISSUES.md`](../KNOWN-ISSUES.md) — 28 issues, rewritten at your
request into open items only, every row checked against the tree rather than copied forward.** Read
that first; this section is just what moved overnight. Detail is in the dated sections below.

### Two real defects found and fixed, both in shipping code

* **C3 — `umount` during I/O destroyed the data and returned success.** A 200 MiB write, unmounted
  2 s in, lost everything past 5 MiB while `umount` reported `rc=0`. Two drain-based fixes failed
  before the right answer: the writer holds the file **open**, so the in-flight count is legitimately
  zero between writes. libext2 already knew the answer — `ext2_open()` takes a reference it never
  releases — so umass now returns **`-EBUSY`**, which is what Linux does. Gated from a pristine
  filesystem: refused promptly, all 200 MiB written, `e2fsck` rc=0.
* **C7 — the SD driver's ADMA2 engine silently killed WiFi, for three weeks.** `_sdio_admaArm()`
  wrote the low half of the 64-bit ADMA address and left `ADMA_ADDR_2` uninitialised. SD never
  noticed (12.9 MB/s, `e2fsck` clean); the stray bus accesses stopped the BCM43455 firmware loading
  entirely. **One line.** Found by narrowing across four A/B configurations before touching anything.

### Three claims in the docs were wrong, and are corrected

* **C2 is not a kernel DoS.** The stack-exhaustion reproducer no longer takes the box down — 3 boots,
  5 runs, 3 bombs in one boot, the process dies and psh keeps working. The EL1 abort was the **crash
  dumper** reading an unmapped page, now guarded. C2 re-scoped to the latent residual.
* **WiFi has no userspace in the image** — `rpi4-wifi` is built standalone and was never shipped, so
  "just start the daemon" was impossible. Now built against today's tree and staged.
* **Two debt rows were the same item, and the upstream retry budget was misquoted** in a way that
  inverted its meaning (ours is the *same* 500 ms, ten times finer-grained).

### Also shipped

`swprintf`/`vswprintf` in libphoenix (validated differentially against glibc, 11 target tests) ·
`ext2_statfs` no longer leaks a heap pointer as `f_fsid` · quake3 times with `CLOCK_MONOTONIC`, so
the 56-year NTP step can no longer kill its startup · the branch backlog went 22 → 5.

### Both lanes gated on the whole day's work

* **netboot** — six-app showcase **6/6**, 0 faults, 0 allocator guard fires, torches present, and the
  STK and X frames inspected rather than trusted.
* **SD** — card reflashed (`1093+1 records`, 12.5 MB/s) and booted: ext2 root mounted **through
  ADMA2** after 0 tries, `SPARSE TEST OK`, 0 faults. The card's own `build-versions` confirms all
  five SHAs.

### Full libc suite re-gated after the libphoenix changes — and one new intermittent (C7)

Only `test-libc-printf` had been run against today's `swprintf` and allocator-reporter commits, so
the whole suite went on hardware. **stdlib / string / stdio / libcache 432/0. pthread 30/0, signal
32/0, time 43/0.** 0 allocator guard lines, 0 faults. The gate for today's libphoenix work is green.

`test-libc-misc` came back **212 Tests 3 Failures** once — three consecutive cases in
`stat_nlink_size_blk_tim`, each failing at its first resolution of the same name.

↩ **I first called this a regression. It is not, and I withdraw that.** I dated it against a single
